\hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block}{}\section{M\+C\+F\+Block Class Reference}
\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block}\index{M\+C\+F\+Block@{M\+C\+F\+Block}}


implementation of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} concept for the (linear) Min-\/\+Cost Flow problem  




{\ttfamily \#include $<$M\+C\+F\+Block.\+h$>$}

\subsection*{Public Types}
\begin{Indent}\textbf{ Public types}\par
{\em \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block}{M\+C\+F\+Block}} defines four main public types\+:


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item Index, the type of node indices;
\item Index, the type of arc indices;
\item F\+Number, the type of flow variables, arc capacities, and node deficits;
\item C\+Number, the type of flow costs, node potentials, and arc reduced costs;
\item F\+O\+Number, the type of objective function value.
\end{DoxyItemize}

By re-\/defining the types in this section, some (but not all) solution algorithms may be able to work with the \char`\"{}smallest\char`\"{} choice of data type that is capable of properly representing the data of the instances to be solved. This may be relevant due to an important property of M\+CF problems\+: {\itshape if all arc capacities and node deficits are integer, then there exists an integral optimal primal solution}, and {\itshape if all arc costs are integer, then there exists an integral optimal dual solution}. Even more importantly, {\itshape many solution algorithms will in fact produce an integral primal/dual solution for free}, because {\itshape every primal/dual solution they generate during the solution process is naturally integral}. Therefore, one can use integer data types to represent everything connected with flows and/or costs if the corresponding data is integer in all instances one needs to solve. This directly translates in significant memory savings and/or speed improvements.

However, while using a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block}{M\+C\+F\+Block}} as a part of some larger problem, it may be difficult to fully exploit this property\+: even if some \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} can exploit it, not all ofthem may be able to (one example are Interior-\/\+Point approaches, which require both flow and cost variables to be continuous), and maybe some other aspects of the overall solution algorithm will require general double data anyway. One should actually have \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} template over all these types to be able to fully exploit this property, which may be a future evolution but is not what this implementation does. The current choice is to use the \char`\"{}worst case scenario\char`\"{} where F\+Number == C\+Number == O\+F\+Number == double, although the data types are left there and it is therefore in principle possible to change this. Note, however, that the above integrality property only holds for {\itshape linear} M\+CF problems. Should the class be extended, by even allowing arc costs to be convex quadratic (the simplest possible nonlionear extension), then a single arc with a nonzero quadratic cost coefficient implies that optimal flows and potentials may be fractional even if all the data of the problem (comprised quadratic cost coefficients) is integer. Hence, for such a setting F\+Number == C\+Number == O\+F\+Number == double is actually {\itshape mandatory}, for any reasonable algorithm will typically misbehave otherwise. }\begin{DoxyCompactItemize}

\begin{Indent}\textbf{ Changing the data of the M\+CF instabnce}\par
{\em All the methods in this section have two parameters issue\+Mod and issue\+A\+Mod which control if and how the, respectively, \char`\"{}physical Modification\char`\"{} and \char`\"{}abstract Modification\char`\"{} corresponding to the change have to be issued, and where (to which channel). The format of the parameters is that of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_observer_a2d499ae38e5e95cf66cd71da32b0694a}{Observer\+::make\+\_\+par()}}, except that the value e\+Mod\+Blck is ignored and treated it as if it were e\+No\+Blck \mbox{[}see \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_observer_adbb3f266845b94b3cc19a549c2634f3d}{Observer\+::issue\+\_\+pmod()}}\mbox{]}. This is because it makes no sense to issue an \char`\"{}abstract\char`\"{} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} with concerns\+\_\+\+Block() == true, since the changes in the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block}{M\+C\+F\+Block}} have surely been done already, and this is just not possible for a \char`\"{}physical\char`\"{} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}}. }\begin{DoxyCompactItemize}
\item 
void \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a41c883dcdfab5eef37487ea9970cd9ee}{chg\+\_\+costs}} (\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_aa2211b659beb942cfb7618c11033831e}{c\+\_\+\+Vec\+\_\+\+C\+Number\+\_\+it}} N\+Cost, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_aee6156e2ce80720d57a082d80f39d8cc}{c\+\_\+\+Index}} strt=0, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_ad53d6229d7ec28555a9c9c2fd9abf773}{Index}} stop=\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_inf}{Inf}}$<$ \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_ad53d6229d7ec28555a9c9c2fd9abf773}{Index}} $>$(), \mbox{\hyperlink{group___modification___t_y_p_e_s_ga544246d8c462e9a2d817c2c30797968f}{c\+\_\+\+Mod\+Param}} issue\+Mod=\mbox{\hyperlink{group___modification___t_y_p_e_s_gga48fddcdd03a03f49b7e389ca1ed938d4ada69e89c5f46e563d8a13cfa6830c136}{e\+No\+Blck}}, \mbox{\hyperlink{group___modification___t_y_p_e_s_ga544246d8c462e9a2d817c2c30797968f}{c\+\_\+\+Mod\+Param}} issue\+A\+Mod=\mbox{\hyperlink{group___modification___t_y_p_e_s_gga48fddcdd03a03f49b7e389ca1ed938d4ada69e89c5f46e563d8a13cfa6830c136}{e\+No\+Blck}})

\subsection{Detailed Description}
implementation of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} concept for the (linear) Min-\/\+Cost Flow problem 

The \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block}{M\+C\+F\+Block}} class implements the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} concept \mbox{[}see \mbox{\hyperlink{_block_8h}{Block.\+h}}\mbox{]} for the (linear) Min-\/\+Cost Flow (M\+CF) problem.

The data of the problem consist of a (directed) graph G = ( N , A ) with n = $\vert$\+N$\vert$ nodes and m = $\vert$\+A$\vert$ (directed) arcs. Each node `i' has a deficit b\mbox{[} i \mbox{]}, i.\+e., the amount of flow that is produced/consumed by the node\+: source nodes (which produce flow) have negative deficits and sink nodes (which consume flow) have positive deficits. Each arc `(i, j)' has an upper capacity U\mbox{[} i , j \mbox{]} and a linear cost coefficient C\mbox{[} i , j \mbox{]}. Flow variables X\mbox{[} i , j \mbox{]} represents the amount of flow to be sent on arc (i, j). Parallel arcs, i.\+e., multiple copies of the same arc `(i, j)' are in general allowed; it is expected that they have different costs (for otherwise they can be merged into a unique arc), but this is not strictly enforced. Multiple copies of some arc (i, j) can be seen as \char`\"{}total\char`\"{} flow cost on that arc being a piecewise-\/linear convex function.

The formulation of the problem is\+: \[ \min \sum_{ (i, j) \in A } C[ i , j ] X[ i, j ] \] \[ \sum_{ (j, i) \in A } X[ j , i ] - \sum_{ (i, j) \in A } X[ i , j ] = b[ i ] \quad i \in N (1) \] \[ 0 \leq X[ i , j ] \leq U[ i , j ] \quad (i, j) \in A (2) \] The n equations (1) are the flow conservation constraints and the 2m inequalities (2) are the flow nonnegativity and capacity constraints. At least one of the flow conservation constraints is redundant, as the demands must be balanced ( $\sum_{ i \in N } b[ i ] = 0$); indeed, exactly n -\/ Connected\+Components( G ) flow conservation constraints are redundant, as demands must be balanced in each connected component of G.

The dual of the problem is\+: \[ \max \sum_{ i \in N } Pi[ i ] b[ i ] - \sum_{ (i, j) \in A } W[ i , j ] U[ i , j ] - \] \[ Pi[ j ] - Pi[ i ] - W[ i , j ] + Z[ i , j ] = C[ i , j ] \quad (i, j) \in A (3) \] \[ W[ i , j ] \geq 0 \quad (i, j) \in A (4) \] \[ Z[ i , j ] \geq 0 \quad (i, j) \in A (5) \]

Pi\mbox{[}\mbox{]} is said the vector of node potentials for the problem, W\mbox{[}\mbox{]} are bound variables and Z\mbox{[}\mbox{]} are slack variables. Given Pi\mbox{[}\mbox{]}, the quantities \[ RC[ i , j ] = C[ i , j ] + Q[ i , j ] * X[ i , j ] - Pi[ j ] + Pi[ i ] \] are said the \char`\"{}reduced costs\char`\"{} of arcs, and are basically the dual variables of the box constraints.

A primal and dual feasible solution pair is optimal if and only if the complementary slackness conditions \[ RC[ i , j ] > 0 \Rightarrow X[ i , j ] = 0 (6) \] \[ RC[ i , j ] < 0 \Rightarrow X[ i , j ] = U[ i , j ] (7) \] are satisfied for all arcs (i, j) of A.

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{add\+\_\+\+Modification()}{add\_Modification()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily virtual void add\+\_\+\+Modification (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{group___modification___t_y_p_e_s_ga9024cf2ead61a1280d29771b063b1a62}{sp\+\_\+\+Mod}}}]{mod,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_observer_a54ce8db639626e45b1f20c289baa989b}{Chnl\+Name}}}]{chnl = {\ttfamily 0} }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [final]}, {\ttfamily [override]}, {\ttfamily [virtual]}}



adding a new \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} to the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block}{M\+C\+F\+Block}} 

Method for handling \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}}.

The version of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block}{M\+C\+F\+Block}} has to intercept any \char`\"{}abstract Modification\char`\"{} that modifies the \char`\"{}abstract representation\char`\"{} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block}{M\+C\+F\+Block}}, and \char`\"{}translate\char`\"{} them into both changes of the actual data structures and corresponding \char`\"{}physical Modification\char`\"{}.

The following \char`\"{}abstract Modification\char`\"{} are handled\+:


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_group_modification}{Group\+Modification}}, that are simply unpacked into the individual sub-\/\mbox{[}Group\mbox{]}\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} and dealt with individually;
\item \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_linear_function_mod_sbst}{Linear\+Function\+Mod\+Sbst}} adding/removing \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} and changing coefficients coming from the (\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_linear_function}{Linear\+Function}} into the F\+Row)\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}}, but {\itshape not} from the (\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_linear_function}{Linear\+Function}} into the F\+Row)\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}};
\item \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_linear_function_mod_rngd}{Linear\+Function\+Mod\+Rngd}} adding/removing \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} and changing coefficients coming from the (\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_linear_function}{Linear\+Function}} into the F\+Row)\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}}, but {\itshape not} from the (\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_linear_function}{Linear\+Function}} into the F\+Row)\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}};
\item \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_row_constraint_mod}{Row\+Constraint\+Mod}} changing the R\+HS of the bound constraints and both sides at once of the flow conservation ones, but not any other combination (and note that the R\+HS of the bound constraints may not be changeable at all depending on how they have been constructed; in this case attempting to do this throws exception, which means that there is no need to handling this here);
\item \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable_mod}{Variable\+Mod}} fixing and un-\/fixing a flow \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_col_variable}{Col\+Variable}}; however, note that {\itshape fixing is only permitted if the value() of the Col\+Vvariable is zero}, because that corresponds to closing the arc, exception being thrown otherwise.
\end{DoxyItemize}

Any other \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} reaching the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block}{M\+C\+F\+Block}} will lead to exception being thrown. 

Reimplemented from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a9b94536a10555dfb680e015fcb4787a8}{Block}}.

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{bound\+\_\+feasible()}{bound\_feasible()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily bool bound\+\_\+feasible (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a956c123a5a6b2b52f033ca1987acd80e}{c\+\_\+\+F\+Number}}}]{feps,  }\item[{bool}]{useabstract = {\ttfamily false} }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}



returns true if the current solution is (approximately) bound feasible 

Returns true if the solution encoded in the current value of the flow (x) \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block}{M\+C\+F\+Block}} is approximately feasible w.\+r.\+t. the bound constraints only. This clearly requires the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block}{M\+C\+F\+Block}} to have been defined, i.\+e., that \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a599bd50cdc6123555aa4a6c4e22c996d}{generate\+\_\+static\+\_\+variables()}} has been called prior to this method. The parameter feps is the relative accuracy defining \char`\"{}approximately\char`\"{}. The parameter \char`\"{}useabstract\char`\"{} has the same meaning as in \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a162cd7d8375d705f0b15d8e847098763}{is\+\_\+feasible()}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a0b1e7ff87a97e692c97f52f096f6eb50}{is\+\_\+optimal()}}. Of course, if useabstract == true, then the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block}{M\+C\+F\+Block}} need to have been generated by calling \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a33f94035590874ef8616982e11e7bb43}{generate\+\_\+static\+\_\+constraints()}} prior to this method. \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a3d5efc1373ecc4f71e494c915663b6c4}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a3d5efc1373ecc4f71e494c915663b6c4}}

changes the cost of the given arc 

Changes the cost of the given arc.

Note that this can issue only one \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} of each type; the \char`\"{}physical\char`\"{} one is a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_rngd_mod}{M\+C\+F\+Block\+Rngd\+Mod}} with stop = start + 1. \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a41c883dcdfab5eef37487ea9970cd9ee}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a41c883dcdfab5eef37487ea9970cd9ee}}

change the costs of a contiguous interval of arcs 

Method to change the costs of a subset of arcs with \char`\"{}contiguous names\char`\"{}. That is, $\ast$( N\+Cost + i -\/ strt ) becomes the new cost of arc i for all strt $<$= i $<$ min( stop , get\+\_\+\+N\+Arcs() ).

Note that, if the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}} is a \char`\"{}sparse\char`\"{} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_linear_function}{Linear\+Function}} (see compute\+\_\+objective()), then changing the costs can issue up to three different \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}}; in particular a Linear\+Function\+Mod for adding a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} (setting to nonzero a previously zero coefficient), one for removing \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} (vice-\/versa), and one for modifying the coefficients. If more than one \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} is actually issued and issue\+A\+Mod specifies an open channel, then the channel is nested so that the three \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} are grouped into a single \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_group_modification}{Group\+Modification}}. Similarly, if instead issue\+A\+Mod specifies the default channel, then a new channel is opened to group the multiple \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} and immediately closed when the last one is issued. If, instead, the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}} is a \char`\"{}dense\char`\"{} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_linear_function}{Linear\+Function}}, then at most one Linear\+Function\+Mod for modifying the coefficients is issued. Of course this only applies if issue\+A\+Mod specifies that abstract \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} have to be issued {\itshape and} the abstract \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}} has been constructed.

Also, if issue\+Mod says so then a \char`\"{}physical\char`\"{} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_rngd_mod}{M\+C\+F\+Block\+Rngd\+Mod}} is issued. \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_aa5e5b9ed0a813a3e09ef80fe0ee3920c}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_aa5e5b9ed0a813a3e09ef80fe0ee3920c}} 
\index{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+M\+C\+F\+Block@{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+M\+C\+F\+Block}!chg\+\_\+costs@{chg\+\_\+costs}}

change the costs of an arbitrary subset of arcs 

Method to change the costs of an arbitrary subset of arc. That is, $\ast$( N\+Cost + i ) becomes the new cost of arc nms\mbox{[} i \mbox{]} for all 0 $<$= i $<$ N\+Cost.\+size(), (which means that nms.\+size() == N\+Cost.\+size()). The parameter ordered tells if the nms vector is ordered for increasing index of the arc. As the the \&\& tells, nms is \char`\"{}consumed\char`\"{} by the method, typically being shipped to an appropriate \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_sbst_mod}{M\+C\+F\+Block\+Sbst\+Mod}} object.

See chg\+\_\+costs( range ) for \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} issued (except that, of course, the \char`\"{}physical\char`\"{} one is a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_sbst_mod}{M\+C\+F\+Block\+Sbst\+Mod}}). \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a0ad2915af3702e293523fb3ac75ad599}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a0ad2915af3702e293523fb3ac75ad599}}

change the deficits of a contiguous interval of nodes 

Method to change the deficits of a subset of nodes with \char`\"{}contiguous
names\char`\"{}. That is, $\ast$( N\+Dfct + i -\/ strt ) becomes the new deficit of node i for all strt $<$= i $<$ min( stop , get\+\_\+\+N\+Nodes() ). Note that \char`\"{}node names\char`\"{} here go from 0 to \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_afff9080606a5612d33ba20c39d663839}{get\+\_\+\+N\+Nodes()}} -\/ 1, despite the fact that \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a48ce277b5dac05d54aa2281fa4595c7b}{get\+\_\+\+S\+N()}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a34619412f7c801aadfa0b083a6efda36}{get\+\_\+\+E\+N()}} report node \char`\"{}names\char`\"{} between 1 and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_afff9080606a5612d33ba20c39d663839}{get\+\_\+\+N\+Nodes()}}.

Note that changing the capacities can issue as many \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} as there are nodes in the range, in particular \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_f_row_constraint_mod}{F\+Row\+Constraint\+Mod}} with type \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_row_constraint_mod_af91e41a206e988d55a31347acd6f5d33a555ab61cc2e0ed36251cba947d2beccd}{Row\+Constraint\+Mod\+::e\+Chg\+B\+TS}}. If more than one \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} is actually issued and issue\+A\+Mod specifies an open channel, then the channel is nested so that all the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} are grouped into a single \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_group_modification}{Group\+Modification}}. Similarly, if instead issue\+A\+Mod specifies the default channel, then a new channel is opened to group the multiple \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} and immediately closed when the last one is issued. Of course this only applies if issue\+A\+Mod specifies that abstract \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} have to be issued {\itshape and} the abstract \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} have been constructed.

Also, if issue\+Mod says so then a \char`\"{}physical\char`\"{} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_rngd_mod}{M\+C\+F\+Block\+Rngd\+Mod}} is issued. \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a68732575daf2a3d3e2601ca4f5742f39}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a68732575daf2a3d3e2601ca4f5742f39}}

Note that \char`\"{}node names\char`\"{} here go from 0 to \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_afff9080606a5612d33ba20c39d663839}{get\+\_\+\+N\+Nodes()}} -\/ 1, despite the fact that \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a48ce277b5dac05d54aa2281fa4595c7b}{get\+\_\+\+S\+N()}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a34619412f7c801aadfa0b083a6efda36}{get\+\_\+\+E\+N()}} report node \char`\"{}names\char`\"{} between 1 and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_afff9080606a5612d33ba20c39d663839}{get\+\_\+\+N\+Nodes()}}. As the the \&\& tells, nms is \char`\"{}consumed\char`\"{} by the method, typically being shipped to an appropriate \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_sbst_mod}{M\+C\+F\+Block\+Sbst\+Mod}} object.

See chg\+\_\+dfcts( range ) for \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} issued (except that, of course, the \char`\"{}physical\char`\"{} one is a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_sbst_mod}{M\+C\+F\+Block\+Sbst\+Mod}}). \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a1777a59a4297b24604825e498b069741}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a1777a59a4297b24604825e498b069741}}

change the capacity of the given arc 

Method to change the capacity of a given arc\+: N\+Cap becomes the new capacity of arc arc. Note that, according to the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} of the static \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}}, the capacity of the arcs cannot be changed\+: trying to do that will result in an exception being thrown.

Note that this can issue only one \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}}; the \char`\"{}physical\char`\"{} one is a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_rngd_mod}{M\+C\+F\+Block\+Rngd\+Mod}} with stop = start + 1. \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a5ad49ca02b4084f029a45f11e563494f}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a5ad49ca02b4084f029a45f11e563494f}}

change the capacities of a contiguous interval of arcs 

Method to change the capacities of a subset of arcs with \char`\"{}contiguous
names\char`\"{}. That is, $\ast$( N\+Cap + i -\/ strt ) becomes the new capacity of arc i for all strt $<$= i $<$ min( stop , get\+\_\+\+N\+Arcs() ). Note that, according to the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} of the static \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}}, the capacity of the arcs cannot be changed\+: trying to do that will result in an exception being thrown.

Note that changing the capacities can issue as many \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} as there are arcs in the range, in particular \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_one_var_constraint_mod}{One\+Var\+Constraint\+Mod}} with type \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_row_constraint_mod_af91e41a206e988d55a31347acd6f5d33a6c90a5d6f098c2913eef0d4058b65b1f}{Row\+Constraint\+Mod\+::e\+Chg\+R\+HS}}. If more than one \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} is actually issued and issue\+A\+Mod specifies an open channel, then the channel is nested so that all the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} are grouped into a single \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_group_modification}{Group\+Modification}}. Similarly, if instead issue\+A\+Mod specifies the default channel, then a new channel is opened to group the multiple \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} and immediately closed when the last one is issued. Of course this only applies if issue\+A\+Mod specifies that abstract \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} have to be issued {\itshape and} the abstract \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} have been constructed.

Also, if issue\+Mod says so then a \char`\"{}physical\char`\"{} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_rngd_mod}{M\+C\+F\+Block\+Rngd\+Mod}} is issued. \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a521e6ec429dd1558b34f6be23db855a9}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a521e6ec429dd1558b34f6be23db855a9}} 
\index{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+M\+C\+F\+Block@{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+M\+C\+F\+Block}!chg\+\_\+ucaps@{chg\+\_\+ucaps}}
\index{chg\+\_\+ucaps@{chg\+\_\+ucaps}!S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+M\+C\+F\+Block@{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+M\+C\+F\+Block}}

change the capacities of an arbitrary subset of arcs 

Method to change the capacities of an arbitrary subset of arc. That is, $\ast$( N\+Cap + i ) becomes the new capacity of arc nms\mbox{[} i \mbox{]} for all 0 $<$= i $<$ N\+Cap.\+size() (which means that nms.\+size() == N\+Cap.\+size()). The parameter ordered tells if the nms vector is ordered for increasing index of the arc. As the the \&\& tells, nms is \char`\"{}consumed\char`\"{} by the method, typically being shipped to an appropriate \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_sbst_mod}{M\+C\+F\+Block\+Sbst\+Mod}} object.

Note that, according to the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} of the static \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}}, the capacity of the arcs cannot be changed\+: trying to do that will result in an exception being thrown.

See chg\+\_\+ucaps( range ) for \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} issued (except that, of course, the \char`\"{}physical\char`\"{} one is a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_sbst_mod}{M\+C\+F\+Block\+Sbst\+Mod}}). \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a571800e44805781e142094cdcf1f7e21}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a571800e44805781e142094cdcf1f7e21}}

closes the given arc 

Method to \char`\"{}close\char`\"{} the given arc\+: the flow on arc is fixed to 0. The arc is not removed from the problem, and its capacity and cost are not changed, so that it can be easily re-\/opened later. When the problem is created, all arcs are open. Closing an already closed arc does nothing.

Note that this can issue only one \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}}; the \char`\"{}physical\char`\"{} one is a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_rngd_mod}{M\+C\+F\+Block\+Rngd\+Mod}} with stop = start + 1. \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a002a805c2a25efab3dd3b3c5c2659050}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a002a805c2a25efab3dd3b3c5c2659050}}

closes a contiguous interval of arcs 

Method to close a subset of arcs with all \char`\"{}contiguous names\char`\"{} comprised between strt (included) and min( stop , get\+\_\+\+N\+Arcs() ) (excluded). The flow on the arcs is fixed to 0 but the arcs are not removed from the problem, and their capacity and cost are not changed, so that they can be easily re-\/opened later. When the problem is created, all arcs are open. Closing an already closed arc does nothing.

Note that closing multiple arcs can issue as many \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} as there are arcs in the range, in particular \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable_mod}{Variable\+Mod}} with type \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable_a30febf04ce89c19659143fa83ae6f7baaf0cb52fcb65ba1ee2e93ef655d5c7693}{Variable\+::k\+Fixed}}. If more than one \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} is actually issued and issue\+A\+Mod specifies an open channel, then the channel is nested so that all the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} are grouped into a single \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_group_modification}{Group\+Modification}}. Similarly, if instead issue\+A\+Mod specifies the default channel, then a new channel is opened to group the multiple \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} and immediately closed when the last one is issued. Of course this only applies if issue\+A\+Mod specifies that abstract \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} have to be issued.

Also, if issue\+Mod says so then a \char`\"{}physical\char`\"{} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_rngd_mod}{M\+C\+F\+Block\+Rngd\+Mod}} is issued. \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_acc843b2f20a57f8e3380d90c844c1686}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_acc843b2f20a57f8e3380d90c844c1686}}

closes an arbitrary subset of arcs 

Method to close an arbitrary subset of arc, i.\+e., all those whose names are found in the array nms. The flow on the arcs is fixed to 0 but the arcs are not removed from the problem, and their capacity and cost are not changed, so that they can be easily re-\/opened later. When the problem is created, all arcs are open. Closing an already closed arc does nothing.

The parameter ordered tells if the nms vector is ordered for increasing index of the arc. As the the \&\& tells, nms is \char`\"{}consumed\char`\"{} by the method, typically being shipped to an appropriate \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_sbst_mod}{M\+C\+F\+Block\+Sbst\+Mod}} object.

See close\+\_\+arcs( range ) for \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} issued (except that, of course, the \char`\"{}physical\char`\"{} one is a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_sbst_mod}{M\+C\+F\+Block\+Sbst\+Mod}}). \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a7c6d8d4ff78950993ded5d1abfd4fe53}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a7c6d8d4ff78950993ded5d1abfd4fe53}}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{deserialize()}{deserialize()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily virtual void deserialize (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{net\+C\+D\+F\+::\+Nc\+Group \&\&}]{group,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} $\ast$}]{father = {\ttfamily nullptr} }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [override]}, {\ttfamily [virtual]}}



de-\/serialize the current \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} out of net\+C\+D\+F\+::\+Nc\+Group 

Fourth and final level de-\/serialization method\+: takes a net\+C\+D\+F\+::\+Nc\+Group supposedly containing all the information required to de-\/serialize the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} must, starting with the \char`\"{}type\char`\"{} attribute that has to contain the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a738e9dfd6e00507da219e4bd5a3f6be9}{name()}} of the current \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} (and exception should clearly be thrown is this does not happen), and initialize the current \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} out of it. \begin{DoxyVerb} THIS IS THE METHOD TO BE IMPLEMENTED BY DERIVED CLASSES
\end{DoxyVerb}


and in fact it is virtual. The format of the information is clearly that set by the serialize( net\+C\+D\+F\+::\+Nc\+Group ) method of the specific \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} class, and exception should be thrown if anything goes wrong in the process.

Two important notes need be done\+:


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a817e3ce47ea40c14517ec16c6b62b814}{deserialize()}} S\+H\+O\+U\+LD O\+N\+LY BE C\+A\+L\+L\+ED ON AN E\+M\+P\+TY \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} .

A \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} is not supposed to handle the transition between having been previously loaded with some instance data, and then having to clean-\/up everything in order to be re-\/filled with something entirely different. If this need be done, it is cleaner to destroy the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} and construct an entirely new one. A non-\/empty \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} may well decide to throw exception if this is done (although it may not). As a consequence, \begin{DoxyVerb} deserialize() IS NOT EXPECTED TO ISSUE Modification ,
\end{DoxyVerb}


as it is not expected that any \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} is attached to an empty \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}.
\item A\+F\+T\+ER \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a817e3ce47ea40c14517ec16c6b62b814}{deserialize()}} T\+HE \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} IS U\+N-\/\+C\+O\+N\+F\+I\+G\+U\+R\+ED

Although clearly not \char`\"{}empty\char`\"{}, as opposed as \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} fresh out of the factory (see new\+\_\+\+Block( string )), a freshly de-\/serialized \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} is otherwise \char`\"{}in pristine state\char`\"{}\+: the \char`\"{}abstract representation\char`\"{} is not constructed (unless the \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} does this by its own volition), both the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}} and the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_solver_config}{Block\+Solver\+Config}} are not set, and (therefore) there are no \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} attached. The e\+Prob\+File S\+M\+S++ nec\+C\+DF file type is precisely provided for allowing to save {\itshape all} the information required to solve a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} (the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} itself, its \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}} and its \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_solver_config}{Block\+Solver\+Config}}), but de-\/serializing the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} and applying them is still the user\textquotesingle{}s responsibility.
\end{DoxyItemize}

To tally with Block\+::serialize( net\+C\+D\+F\+::\+Nc\+Group ), the base class implementation just (checks if type and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a738e9dfd6e00507da219e4bd5a3f6be9}{name()}} match, and) scans all the child Nc\+Group of the current Nc\+Group, assumes that each one of them is a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} group and de-\/serializes a sub-\/\+Block of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} out of it, in left-\/to-\/right order.

Note that the method has a further \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} $\ast$ argument, that can be used as in the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} constructor to ensure that the \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} has access to all the data structures of its father it needs for correctly initializing itself (if any). Indeed, in the base class implementation (that provides a clean blueprint of how this can be done by specific \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}), the parameter is used to pass \char`\"{}this\char`\"{} to all the sub-\/\+Block. This should be the preferred way to access to such information (provided that, as it probably should, the father \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} has correctly initialized itself before doing its sub-\/\+Block), although note that the sub-\/\+Block can alternatively access the information about the father \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} by peeking into the \char`\"{}father\char`\"{} Nc\+Group via Nc\+Group\+::get\+Parent\+Group(). 

Reimplemented from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a4b98fbc561cffa3e92a2690c831ab3c9}{Block}}.

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{generate\+\_\+objective()}{generate\_objective()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily virtual void generate\+\_\+objective (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} $\ast$}]{objc = {\ttfamily nullptr} }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [final]}, {\ttfamily [override]}, {\ttfamily [virtual]}}



generate the objective of the M\+CF 

Method that generates objectivet of the M\+CF. Although this would seem to be an exceedingly simple object, there is still a nontrivial decision to be made about it, i.\+e., whether it is represented as a \char`\"{}sparse\char`\"{} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_linear_function}{Linear\+Function}} or a \char`\"{}dense\char`\"{} one. This is governed by objc\+: if flow conservation equations and the bound constraints. The latter have fixed 0 L\+HS and a generic R\+HS, which can be Inf$<$\+Fnumber$>$(), in this case, it is possible to use \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_n_n_constraint}{N\+N\+Constraint}} to represent them instead of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_l_b0_constraint}{L\+B0\+Constraint}}. The parameter stcc is used to decide if this is done\+: if


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item either objc is not nullptr and it is a Simple\+Configuration$<$double$>$;
\item or f\+\_\+\+Block\+Config is not nullptr, f\+\_\+\+Block\+Config-\/$>$f\+\_\+objective\+\_\+\+Configuration is not nullptr, and it is a Simple\+Configuration$<$double$>$;
\end{DoxyItemize}

then the f\+\_\+value of the Simple\+Configuration$<$int$>$ is taken as the \char`\"{}sparsity
parameter\char`\"{} (sprs) of the objective function; otherwise, sprs == 0. The parameter is used as follows\+: if the number of nonzero arc cost coefficients (at the time in which the method is called) is $>$= sprs $\ast$ \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a42c106aa81abe650c7baa0da547ade7e}{get\+\_\+\+N\+Arcs()}}, then the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_linear_function}{Linear\+Function}} in the objective is created \char`\"{}dense\char`\"{}\+: each flow \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} is \char`\"{}active\char`\"{} in it, even if it has a zero cost coefficient. Otherwise, the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_linear_function}{Linear\+Function}} in the objective is created \char`\"{}sparse\char`\"{}\+: only flow \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} with nonzero coefficient are \char`\"{}active\char`\"{} in it. A \char`\"{}dense\char`\"{} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}} makes it much easier to change the cost coefficients (see chg\+\_\+cost\mbox{[}s\mbox{]}()), but it comes at the cost of more memory. Besides, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} using it and \char`\"{}trusting\char`\"{} the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block}{M\+C\+F\+Block}} about how many nonzeroes are there in the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_linear_function}{Linear\+Function}} may be sorely disappointed, which may have adverse effects on efficiency.

Yet, a \char`\"{}dense\char`\"{} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}} is the default, as with sprs == 0 the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}} is created \char`\"{}dense\char`\"{} even if all arc cost coefficients are zero.

Note that the decision is taken at the moment in which this method is called, and never changed later, even if the number of nonzeroes changes dramatically. Also, note that if all cost coefficients are \char`\"{}naturally\char`\"{} nonzero, then the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}} will be \char`\"{}dense\char`\"{} no matter what the value of sprs is. Although this may seem obvious, this also means that the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}} will remain \char`\"{}dense\char`\"{} even if later on many coefficients become zero. 

Reimplemented from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_ae14038d64cf6067f15cf3f53fd7af9e0}{Block}}.

generate the static constraint of the M\+CF 

Method that generates the static constraint of the M\+CF. These are the flow conservation equations and the bound constraints. The latter have fixed 0 L\+HS and a generic R\+HS, which can be Inf$<$\+Fnumber$>$(). If {\itshape all} the R\+HS are +\+Infty, it is possible to use a std\+::vector$<$\+N\+N\+Constraint$>$ to represent them instead of a std\+::vector$<$\+L\+B0\+Constraint$>$. The parameter stcc is used to decide if this is done\+: if


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item all the R\+HS are +\+Infty;
\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item either stcc is not nullptr and it is a Simple\+Configuration$<$int$>$;
\end{DoxyItemize}
\item or f\+\_\+\+Block\+Config is not nullptr, f\+\_\+\+Block\+Config-\/$>$f\+\_\+static\+\_\+constraints\+\_\+\+Configuration is not nullptr, and it is a Simple\+Configuration$<$int$>$;
\item the f\+\_\+value of the Simple\+Configuration$<$int$>$ is != 0
\end{DoxyItemize}

then \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_n_n_constraint}{N\+N\+Constraint}} are used to implement {\itshape all} bound constraint. Note that this {\itshape makes it impossible to change any R\+HS}, as this would require changing the static \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} and this is not allowed. Indeed, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_n_n_constraint}{N\+N\+Constraint}} throws exception if one tries to change its R\+HS (and L\+HS as well, but this also \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_n_n_constraint}{N\+N\+Constraint}} does). Hence, if the abstract \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} are constructed, changing the R\+HS is not allowed. 

Reimplemented from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_add9b4c918157bda66b5183f67953d49b}{Block}}.

\mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a599bd50cdc6123555aa4a6c4e22c996d}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a599bd50cdc6123555aa4a6c4e22c996d}} 
\index{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+M\+C\+F\+Block@{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+M\+C\+F\+Block}!generate\+\_\+static\+\_\+variables@{generate\+\_\+static\+\_\+variables}}
\index{generate\+\_\+static\+\_\+variables@{generate\+\_\+static\+\_\+variables}!S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+M\+C\+F\+Block@{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+M\+C\+F\+Block}}
\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{generate\+\_\+static\+\_\+variables()}{generate\_static\_variables()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily virtual void generate\+\_\+static\+\_\+variables (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} $\ast$}]{stvv = {\ttfamily nullptr} }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [final]}, {\ttfamily [override]}, {\ttfamily [virtual]}}



generate the \char`\"{}abstract representation\char`\"{} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} 

This method serves is to ensure that the \char`\"{}abstract representation\char`\"{} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}}, be they static or dynamic, of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} is initialized, so that it can be read with \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a6ca8b345bb506a8ac45eef39887bebea}{get\+\_\+static\+\_\+variables()}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a9c17adfb0c344b44e163d40f7fc6926f}{get\+\_\+dynamic\+\_\+variables()}} \mbox{[}see below\mbox{]}. For the dynamic ones this may (or may not) imply that the lists are empty, with the dynamic generation being done into \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_ab90d0f60518eee75370e29c95bae4522}{generate\+\_\+dynamic\+\_\+variables()}} \mbox{[}see below\mbox{]}.

This method has to be used (rather, {\itshape not} used) with caution, because the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} of a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} are in general \char`\"{}thought to be always there\char`\"{}. Indeed, the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} of a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} are \char`\"{}the interface between the Solver and the user
of the Block\char`\"{}, since they are where the solution information is written. However, specialized \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} may have more compact/efficient ways to represent their solution information (say, in a T\+SP one could use n integers describing the permutation rather than n$^\wedge$2 binaries), which a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} may exploit and that a user of that specialized \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} may read through the specialized interface. Thus, if only using a \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} via its specialized interface, the \char`\"{}abstract representation\char`\"{} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can be avoided.

Furthermore, not all \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} of a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} may be \char`\"{}equally important\char`\"{}. For instance, only a (maybe, quite small) subset of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} may be required for certain uses of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, which however logically also has a (much larger) set of \char`\"{}auxiliary\char`\"{} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}}. If the latter are not needed by any of the Solver/users interacting with the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, it is possible to avoid to construct them at all. This is somehow related to, albeit different from, the fact that certain \char`\"{}types\char`\"{} of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} may be \char`\"{}many\char`\"{}, and therefore necessarily have to be generated dynamicallt, see \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_ab90d0f60518eee75370e29c95bae4522}{generate\+\_\+dynamic\+\_\+variables()}} below.

Note, however, that all \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} appearing in any (\char`\"{}abstract
representation\char`\"{} of a) \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} that is explicitly constructed \mbox{[}see \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a33f94035590874ef8616982e11e7bb43}{generate\+\_\+static\+\_\+constraints()}} above\mbox{]} must obviously be constructed, and this {\itshape before} the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} is. Of course, a derived class can ignore all this and just construct them all right away, but this may be work and memory wasted if no \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} actually uses them. Any \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} should ensure that this method has been called at least once before making any attempt at using the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}}, unless it knows for sure that the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} it is working with has done that already. Note that it is easy to check whether or not \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a599bd50cdc6123555aa4a6c4e22c996d}{generate\+\_\+static\+\_\+variables()}} still has to be called by just testing if \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a6ca8b345bb506a8ac45eef39887bebea}{get\+\_\+static\+\_\+variables()}}.size() == 0, although this is a necessary but not sufficient condition, as a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} may not have any \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} at all.

Most expected uses of this method rely on the fact that a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can have several different types (groups) of static \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}}. This is why the method has the parameter stvv, which is a pointer to an arbitrarily complex \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} object. The actual parameter may be of any specific derived class from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}}, and contain all the information that specifies which of the types (groups) of static \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} must actually be constructed. If the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} has sub-\/\+Block, then the \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} object should contain an appropriate \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} object for each of these.

Note that the stcc parameter is meant as an {\itshape override} of the default \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} for this task set by means of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a18d411b740a47db00d15083c5c7ef2ba}{set\+\_\+\+Block\+Config()}}, which means that if the method is called with stcc = nullptr then the corresponding configuration from the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}} is used. Indeed, the method is not pure virtual, but it is given a default implementation doing nothing but calling itself for each inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} with no (= default = nullptr) argument. This is correct for those \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} that either have no static \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} at all or that construct them all anyway, and for which all the inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} either have the same property or are only to be called with the default configuration set by the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}}. Note that if the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}} is not set (nullptr) or the corresponding field is not set (nullptr), this is assumed to mean \char`\"{}construct all the static Variable
that you have, if any\char`\"{}. Thus, the default implementation automatically works in the \char`\"{}simple\char`\"{} cases, while for any other case the \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} will have to implement its own version, say \char`\"{}unpacking\char`\"{} its \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} object to specific sub-\/\+Configuration for each of its sub-\/\+Block. This cannot be done in the default implementation because the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} stvv for the father \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} will likely have to be \char`\"{}unpacked\char`\"{}, with each inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} getting its own specific sub-\/\+Configuration, but this can only be done by a specific \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} for a specific \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}}, as the base \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} class does not have direct support for the fact that a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} contains a sub-\/\+Configuration for each specific sub-\/\+Block of a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. 

Reimplemented from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_acd79fd0980af547f2f06241cb989094e}{Block}}.

Returns a const reference to the vector of node deficits. Note that the returned vector can either be of size \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_afff9080606a5612d33ba20c39d663839}{get\+\_\+\+N\+Nodes()}} or be of size 0, in which case all node deficits are assumed to be 0. Also, note that the position i (0 $<$= i $<$ \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_afff9080606a5612d33ba20c39d663839}{get\+\_\+\+N\+Nodes()}}) in this vector correspond to the node whose name is i + 1 as returned from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a48ce277b5dac05d54aa2281fa4595c7b}{get\+\_\+\+S\+N()}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a34619412f7c801aadfa0b083a6efda36}{get\+\_\+\+E\+N()}}. \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a1e467673e30479a02c09ca7f5c9dc925}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a1e467673e30479a02c09ca7f5c9dc925}}

gets a contiguous interval of the potential solution 

Method to get the potential solution; upon return, P\+Sol\mbox{[} i \mbox{]} contains the current value of the potential solution for node strt + i for all 0 $<$= i $<$ min( stp , get\+\_\+\+N\+Nodes() ). Note that \char`\"{}node names\char`\"{} here go from 0 to \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_afff9080606a5612d33ba20c39d663839}{get\+\_\+\+N\+Nodes()}} -\/ 1, despite the fact that \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a48ce277b5dac05d54aa2281fa4595c7b}{get\+\_\+\+S\+N()}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a34619412f7c801aadfa0b083a6efda36}{get\+\_\+\+E\+N()}} report node \char`\"{}names\char`\"{} between 1 and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_afff9080606a5612d33ba20c39d663839}{get\+\_\+\+N\+Nodes()}}. \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_ab1ec41f3f3cce072e80fdd7887884049}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_ab1ec41f3f3cce072e80fdd7887884049}}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{get\+\_\+\+Solution()}{get\_Solution()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily virtual \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solution}{Solution}}$\ast$ get\+\_\+\+Solution (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} $\ast$}]{solc = {\ttfamily nullptr},  }\item[{bool}]{emptys = {\ttfamily true} }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [final]}, {\ttfamily [override]}, {\ttfamily [virtual]}}



returns a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_solution}{M\+C\+F\+Solution}} representing the current solution of this \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block}{M\+C\+F\+Block}} 

Returns a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_solution}{M\+C\+F\+Solution}} representing the current solution status of this M\+C\+F\+Block.\+The parameter solc decides which part of the solution is saved\+:


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item if solc != nullptr and it is a Simple\+Configuration$<$int$>$, then it depends on solc-\/$>$f\+\_\+value\+:

= 1 means \char`\"{}only map the primal solution\char`\"{}

= 2 means \char`\"{}only map the dual solution\char`\"{}

= everything else (e.\+g., 0) means \char`\"{}map everything\char`\"{};
\item if solc == nullptr, f\+\_\+\+Block\+Config != nullptr, f\+\_\+\+Block\+Config-\/$>$f\+\_\+is\+\_\+feasible\+\_\+\+Configuration != nullptr and it is a Simple\+Configuration$<$int$>$, then it depends on its f\+\_\+value as in the previous case;
\item otherwise, everything (both the primal and the dual solution) is mapped.
\end{DoxyItemize}

Note that \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block}{M\+C\+F\+Block}} may not contain some or all of the required solution, if the corresponding Variable/\+Constraint have not been constructed yet\+: this throws an exception, unless emptys = true, in which case thew \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_solution}{M\+C\+F\+Solution}} object is only prepped for getting a solution, but it is not really getting one now.

Note that, although the method clearly returns a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_solution}{M\+C\+F\+Solution}}, formally the return type is \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solution}{Solution}} $\ast$. This is because it is not possible to forward declare \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_solution}{M\+C\+F\+Solution}} as a derived class from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solution}{Solution}}, nor to define \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_solution}{M\+C\+F\+Solution}} before \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block}{M\+C\+F\+Block}} because the former uses some type information declared in the latter. 

Reimplemented from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a1db67c2f65111af4314e79dfc81ba597}{Block}}.

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{is\+\_\+feasible()}{is\_feasible()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily virtual bool is\+\_\+feasible (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{bool}]{useabstract = {\ttfamily false},  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} $\ast$}]{fsbc = {\ttfamily nullptr} }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [final]}, {\ttfamily [override]}, {\ttfamily [virtual]}}



returns true if the current solution is approximately feasible 

Returns true if the solution encoded in the current value of the flow (x) \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block}{M\+C\+F\+Block}} is approximately feasible. This clearly requires the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block}{M\+C\+F\+Block}} to have been defined, i.\+e., that \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a599bd50cdc6123555aa4a6c4e22c996d}{generate\+\_\+static\+\_\+variables()}} has been called prior to this method. Also, if useabstract == true, then the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block}{M\+C\+F\+Block}} need to have been generated by calling \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a33f94035590874ef8616982e11e7bb43}{generate\+\_\+static\+\_\+constraints()}} prior to this method.

The parameter for deciding what \char`\"{}approximately feasible\char`\"{} exactly means is a single F\+Number value, representing the {\itshape relative} tolerance for satisfaction of both flow conservation constraint and flow upper/lower bounds. This value is to be found as\+:


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item if fsbc is not nullptr and it is a Simple\+Configuration$<$\+F\+Number$>$, then it if fsbc-\/$>$f\+\_\+value;
\item otherwise, if f\+\_\+\+Block\+Config is not nullptr, f\+\_\+\+Block\+Config-\/$>$f\+\_\+is\+\_\+feasible\+\_\+\+Configuration is not nullptr and it is a Simple\+Configuration$<$\+F\+Number$>$, then it is f\+\_\+\+Block\+Config-\/$>$f\+\_\+is\+\_\+feasible\+\_\+\+Configuration-\/$>$f\+\_\+value;
\item otherwise, it is 0. 
\end{DoxyItemize}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{is\+\_\+optimal()}{is\_optimal()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily virtual bool is\+\_\+optimal (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{bool}]{useabstract = {\ttfamily false},  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} $\ast$}]{optc = {\ttfamily nullptr} }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [final]}, {\ttfamily [override]}, {\ttfamily [virtual]}}



returns true if the current solution is (approximately) optimal 

Returns true if the solution encoded in the current value of the flow (x) \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block}{M\+C\+F\+Block}} is approximately optimal, which means that it is approximately feasible, that the dual solution encoded in the current value of the dual multipliers of both the flow conservation and bound constraints is approximately feasible, and that the two approximately satisfies the Complementary Slackness Conditions. This clearly requires that both the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} and the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block}{M\+C\+F\+Block}} to have been defined, i.\+e., that \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a599bd50cdc6123555aa4a6c4e22c996d}{generate\+\_\+static\+\_\+variables()}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a33f94035590874ef8616982e11e7bb43}{generate\+\_\+static\+\_\+constraints()}} have been called prior to this method.

This requires two parameters for deciding what \char`\"{}approximately feasible\char`\"{} means, one for the primal (feps) and one for the dual (ceps), like in \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a7c6d8d4ff78950993ded5d1abfd4fe53}{complementary\+\_\+slackness()}}. These are found as follows\+:


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item if optc is not nullptr and it is a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_simple_configuration}{Simple\+Configuration}}$<$ std\+::pair$<$\+C\+Number,\+F\+Number$>$ $>$, then ceps = optc-\/$>$f\+\_\+value.\+first and feps = optc-\/$>$f\+\_\+value.\+second;
\item if optc is not nullptr and it is a Simple\+Configuration$<$\+C\+Number$>$, then ceps = optc-\/$>$f\+\_\+value, while feps is taken out of f\+\_\+\+Block\+Config-\/$>$f\+\_\+is\+\_\+feasible\+\_\+\+Configuration as in \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a162cd7d8375d705f0b15d8e847098763}{is\+\_\+feasible()}};
\item otherwise, if f\+\_\+\+Block\+Config is not nullptr, then feps is taken out of f\+\_\+\+Block\+Config-\/$>$f\+\_\+is\+\_\+feasible\+\_\+\+Configuration, while ceps is taken out of f\+\_\+\+Block\+Config-\/$>$f\+\_\+is\+\_\+optimal\+\_\+\+Configuration assuming the latter is a Simple\+Configuration$<$\+C\+Number$>$;
\item otherwise, ceps == feps == 0. 
\end{DoxyItemize}

Reimplemented from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a44ef42401ecae613574f3df619480de1}{Block}}.

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{load()}{load()}\hspace{0.1cm}{\footnotesize\ttfamily [1/2]}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily virtual void load (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_aee6156e2ce80720d57a082d80f39d8cc}{c\+\_\+\+Index}}}]{n,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a03461123d4f6090aefeb97ef653d3f2b}{c\+\_\+\+Vec\+\_\+\+Index}} \&}]{p\+En,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a03461123d4f6090aefeb97ef653d3f2b}{c\+\_\+\+Vec\+\_\+\+Index}} \&}]{p\+Sn,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_ae74990406673bbcdf64e1a3e3b680d5c}{c\+\_\+\+Vec\+\_\+\+F\+Number}} \&}]{pU = {\ttfamily \{\}},  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_ad42538e79f9abbdf18061bccc05edcd6}{c\+\_\+\+Vec\+\_\+\+C\+Number}} \&}]{pC = {\ttfamily \{\}},  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_ae74990406673bbcdf64e1a3e3b680d5c}{c\+\_\+\+Vec\+\_\+\+F\+Number}} \&}]{pB = {\ttfamily \{\}} }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [virtual]}}



loads the M\+CF instance from memory 

Loads the M\+CF instance from memory. The parameters are what you expect\+:


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item n is the number of nodes of the network
\item p\+Sn is the vector of the arc starting nodes;
\item p\+En is the vector of the arc ending nodes;
\item pU is the vector of the arc upper capacities; capacities must be nonnegative, but can be infinite; if pU is empty, then all capacities are taken to be infinite;
\item pC is the vector of the arc costs; if pC is empty, then all arc costs are taken to be 0;
\item pB is the vector of the node deficits; source nodes have negative deficits and sink nodes have positive deficits; if pB is empty, then all deficits are taken to be 0 (a circulation problem).
\end{DoxyItemize}

The number m of arcs of the graph need not be explicitly provided because it is the length of p\+En and s\+En; while pU and pC can be empty, they cannot (unless the graph is empty of arcs). Conversely, n must be explicitly provided because the only n-\/vector is pB, which can be empty. \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a221601b23896f7a98c6914f42224d446}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a221601b23896f7a98c6914f42224d446}} 
\index{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+M\+C\+F\+Block@{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+M\+C\+F\+Block}!load@{load}}
\index{load@{load}!S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+M\+C\+F\+Block@{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+M\+C\+F\+Block}}
\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{load()}{load()}\hspace{0.1cm}{\footnotesize\ttfamily [2/2]}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily virtual void load (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{std\+::istream \&}]{input }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [final]}, {\ttfamily [override]}, {\ttfamily [protected]}, {\ttfamily [virtual]}}



loads the M\+CF instance from file in D\+I\+M\+A\+CS standard format 

Protected method for loading a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block}{M\+C\+F\+Block}} out of a std\+::istream (which is what operator$>$$>$ is dispatched to. The std\+::istream is assumed to contain the description of a M\+CF instance in D\+I\+M\+A\+CS standard format, which is the following. The first line must be \begin{DoxyVerb} p min <number of nodes> <number of arcs>
\end{DoxyVerb}


Then the node definition lines must be found, in the form \begin{DoxyVerb} n <node number> <node supply>
\end{DoxyVerb}


Not all nodes need have a node definition line; these are given zero supply, i.\+e., they are transhipment nodes (supplies are the inverse of deficits, i.\+e., a node with positive supply is a source node). Finally, the arc definition lines must be found, in the form

a $<$start node$>$=\char`\"{}\char`\"{}$>$ $<$end node$>$=\char`\"{}\char`\"{}$>$ $<$lower bound$>$=\char`\"{}\char`\"{}$>$ $<$upper bound$>$=\char`\"{}\char`\"{}$>$ $<$flow cost$>$=\char`\"{}\char`\"{}$>$

There must be exactly $<$number of=\char`\"{}\char`\"{} arcs$>$=\char`\"{}\char`\"{}$>$ arc definition lines in the file.

Note that the file format accepted by Load\+M\+CF is more general than the D\+I\+M\+A\+CS standard format, in that node and arc definitions can be mixed in any order, while the D\+I\+M\+A\+CS file requires all node information to appear before all arc information. Also, capacities of arcs can be set to +\+Inf$<$\+F\+Number$>$() by putting \char`\"{}\+I\+N\+F\char`\"{}, \char`\"{}\+Inf\char`\"{} or \char`\"{}inf\char`\"{} in the file (actually, any string starting with \char`\"{}\+I\char`\"{} or \char`\"{}i\char`\"{} where these would be expected. 

Implements \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a27ae9e4279482d52342c7958c34a43c6}{Block}}.

{\footnotesize\ttfamily virtual void map\+\_\+back\+\_\+solution (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} $\ast$}]{R3B,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} $\ast$}]{r3bc = {\ttfamily nullptr},  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} $\ast$}]{solc = {\ttfamily nullptr} }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [final]}, {\ttfamily [override]}, {\ttfamily [virtual]}}



maps back the solution from a copy \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block}{M\+C\+F\+Block}} to the current one 

Maps back the solution from a copy \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block}{M\+C\+F\+Block}} to the current one. The parameter r3bc is useless (has to be nullptr). The parameter solc decides which part of the solution is mapped\+:


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item if solc != nullptr and it is a Simple\+Configuration$<$int$>$, then it depends on solc-\/$>$f\+\_\+value\+:

= 1 means \char`\"{}only map the primal solution\char`\"{}

= 2 means \char`\"{}only map the dual solution\char`\"{}

= everything else (e.\+g., 0) means \char`\"{}map everything\char`\"{};
\item if solc == nullptr, f\+\_\+\+Block\+Config != nullptr, f\+\_\+\+Block\+Config-\/$>$f\+\_\+is\+\_\+feasible\+\_\+\+Configuration != nullptr and it is a Simple\+Configuration$<$int$>$, then it depends on its f\+\_\+value as in the previous case;
\item otherwise, everything (both the primal and the dual solution) is mapped.
\end{DoxyItemize}

Note that R3B may not contain some or all of the required solution, if the corresponding Variable/\+Constraint have not been constructed yet\+: this throws an exception. 

Reimplemented from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a04f0a8a608fcc3ded227c2552f67f1a3}{Block}}.

re-\/opens the given arc 

Method to \char`\"{}open\char`\"{} the given closed arc, i.\+e., allow the flow on arc to vary. Opening an already open arc (which is what all arcs are when the problem is created) does nothing.

Note that this can issue only one \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}}; the \char`\"{}physical\char`\"{} one is a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_rngd_mod}{M\+C\+F\+Block\+Rngd\+Mod}} with stop = start + 1. \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_aabe56fa7e54eaee26b179424027c01bf}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_aabe56fa7e54eaee26b179424027c01bf}}

re-\/opens a contiguous interval of arcs 

Method to \char`\"{}open\char`\"{} a subset of closed arcs with all \char`\"{}contiguous names\char`\"{} comprised between strt (included) and min( stop , get\+\_\+\+N\+Arcs() ) (excluded). Opening an already open arc (which is what all arcs are when the problem is created) does nothing.

Note that opening multiple arcs can issue as many \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} as there are arcs in the range, in particular \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable_mod}{Variable\+Mod}} with type \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_col_variable_ae6a9c27f20ec8030a1aef0c5b655c4e1abe6ad916b46098fac21a307ea72f206e}{Col\+Variable\+::k\+Continuous}}. If more than one \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} is actually issued and issue\+A\+Mod specifies an open channel, then the channel is nested so that all the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} are grouped into a single \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_group_modification}{Group\+Modification}}. Similarly, if instead issue\+A\+Mod specifies the default channel, then a new channel is opened to group the multiple \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} and immediately closed when the last one is issued. Of course this only applies if issue\+A\+Mod specifies that abstract \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}}.

Also, if issue\+Mod says so then a \char`\"{}physical\char`\"{} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_rngd_mod}{M\+C\+F\+Block\+Rngd\+Mod}} is issued. \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a374dc8a46c0ec47bbbfb5b6c02b2b285}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a374dc8a46c0ec47bbbfb5b6c02b2b285}}

re-\/opens an arbitrary subset of arcs 

Method to \char`\"{}open\char`\"{} an arbitrary subset of closed arc, i.\+e., all those whose names are found in the array nms. Opening an already open arc (which is what all arcs are when the problem is created) does nothing.

The parameter ordered tells if the nms vector is ordered for increasing index of the arc. As the the \&\& tells, nms is \char`\"{}consumed\char`\"{} by the method, typically being shipped to an appropriate \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_sbst_mod}{M\+C\+F\+Block\+Sbst\+Mod}} object.

Note that closing multiple arcs can issue as many \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} as there are arcs in the range, in particular \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable_mod}{Variable\+Mod}} with type \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable_a30febf04ce89c19659143fa83ae6f7baaf0cb52fcb65ba1ee2e93ef655d5c7693}{Variable\+::k\+Fixed}}. If more than one \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} is actually issued and issue\+A\+Mod specifies an open channel, then the channel is nested so that all the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} are grouped into a single \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_group_modification}{Group\+Modification}}. Similarly, if instead issue\+A\+Mod specifies the default channel, then a new channel is opened to group the multiple \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} and immediately closed when the last one is issued. Of course this only applies if issue\+A\+Mod specifies that abstract \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} have to be issued {\itshape and} the abstract \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} have been constructed.

Also, if issue\+Mod says so then a \char`\"{}physical\char`\"{} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_rngd_mod}{M\+C\+F\+Block\+Rngd\+Mod}} is issued. \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a617885c1d228e0ca67bbb0efdbe713e3}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a617885c1d228e0ca67bbb0efdbe713e3}}
